**release authors:**

BA.9 is written as one task in three parts. Part A is the release plumbing: order-independent, codable now, and committed by itself. Parts B and C are the stamp, invocation, collect and gauntlet. They start only when every other §L task is ✅ (GR-2), so a v1.70 stamp is never burned and no broken web build is ever deployed early. If GR-2 is unmet, BA.9 commits Part A, marks itself `[/]` and stops. That is the rubric's execution doctrine, not a coding blocker. I named the operator tasks OP.8–OP.12 so they don't collide with other groups' OP.n numbering; renumber them freely.

I found sibling-partial conflicts that BA.9 must resolve to be codable. The task states the resolutions inline as "Cross-partial resolutions (binding for this task)". They need confirmation when you merge, and the sibling tasks must agree:

1. **STAGE_ORDER.** L-content §3.2 and its entity row want the stage names `modules-stage` and `modules-deploy`, inserted between `web-deploy` and `linux`. L-release §2.2 has a closed set of 11 stages and 17 pairs, with `modules-deploy` placed before `web-deploy` and no `modules-stage`.
   - Resolution: L-release's order. `modules-stage.mjs` runs as the second command of the `inputs` stage.
   - BA.1 must not expect a separate stage.

2. **`scripts/modules-deploy.mjs` ownership.** L-release §4 assigns it to BA.9; L-content §4 assigns it to BA.1. They also differ on transport: scp plus `mv -n` versus rsync `--ignore-existing`. L-content's entity cell also cites a stale `SAM_ASSET_HOST_RSYNC` key.
   - Resolution: BA.9 is the sole owner and implements both partials' duties. BA.1's task must drop this file.

3. **Model mirror URLs.** L-native-embed §2.4 publishes `/models/sha256/…` and `/models/manifest.json` on the bare host. L-release §2.14 and the standing rule require the `https://sanctissimissa.robin.mba/runtime/` prefix.
   - Resolution: `/runtime/models/sha256/…`. BA.12's manifest rows and `EMBED_MODEL_ASSETS.mirrorUrl` must use the `/runtime/` prefix, or `modules-deploy` fails closed.

4. **wllama.** L-release §2.7 and row L.7-11e forbid any wllama file in the web output, and §2.6 closes `public/` to 3 files. L-native-embed F6 bundles wllama same-origin at `dist-web/vendor/wllama/3.8.1/`, which is technically needed because worker scripts must be same-origin.
   - Resolution: admit exactly the three manifest-pinned wllama files. L.7-11e is reworded accordingly.

5. **Corpus pointer.** L-release uses `VENDORED/corpus/CURRENT.json`, with `sync-db` overwriting `assets/missal.db`, and keeps `inputHashes` closed at 2 keys. L-content uses `assets/corpus.current.json` with `corpus-current.mjs` and `build.rs` `SAM_CORPUS_PATH`, treats `assets/missal.db` as read-only forever, and adds pointer-based `inputHashes` keys.
   - Resolution: both pointers exist and must agree on sha256. BA.9 never writes `assets/missal.db`, per the "never mutate corpus in place" rule. The `sync-db` and `build.rs` edits belong to BA.1. `inputHashes` follows L-content when its pointer exists, otherwise the legacy keys.
   - The corpus_version grammar accepts the optional `-q3e256` suffix.

6. **LFS.** L-release §2.6 and F1 require Forgejo LFS, while the §6 codemap in the same partial says LFS is not in use now. `.lfsconfig` still points at forgejo.robin.mba, and the Admin-Manual `origin` is also Forgejo.
   - BA.9 adds the `.gitattributes` rules but always pushes with `--no-verify`, which skips the LFS upload. The Admin-Manual push goes only to its `github` remote.
   - L.7-11g uses a local `file://` LFS transport until OP.10 is done.
   - Note that GGUFs (~640 MB) and the corpus (>100 MB) cannot be regular GitHub blobs.

7. **`rpm`.** The partial says `rpm` is installed on the Ubuntu host, but it is not (`which rpm` finds nothing). BA.9 falls back to the container, and OP.12 is optional.

8. **File-size rule.** `scripts/release-state.mjs` is 787 lines and `collect-artifacts.mjs` is 505. BA.9 extracts `scripts/release-lock.mjs`, `release-stage-commands.mjs` and `release-stage-outputs.mjs` to bring both under 500.
   - `release-state.d.mts` keeps `STAGE_ORDER: readonly string[]`, because `tsconfig` includes `tests/` and `tests/releaseState.test.ts` must still typecheck. That test file is untouched and is not a verification surface.

9. **Rubric rows.**
   - The new section heading `## L — Bookstore…` coexists with the existing `## L — Lore callouts` (ids `L-n` versus `L.7-n`).
   - The audiobook partial's 20 entity rows were condensed into the 13 sub-rows L.7-6a…6m it asked for.
   - L.7-10a and L.7-10b (native engine active; reranker top-5 order parity) are architect-authored from L-native-embed's acceptances and D2's parity rationale. No partial gives exact parity queries, so the run report fixes three queries before the run.
   - L.7-13 author/translator wording, "St. Thomas Aquinas" and "Newman (Oxford translation)", is inferred from L-commerce's summary "Oxford 1841–1845 / Newman". Confirm the exact strings against the BA.5 provenance source.

10. **Evidence-directory grammar.** I refined it to `v<VERSION>-<platform>-buildenv-<env>-<UTC>` so the dual-env runs don't collide.

11. **Web baseline.** `version.json` is not in `dist-web`, so the F5 baseline is recorded from About › Version & Build plus the sha256 of the live `sw.js`.
